commit 5740a2a63eaea76572da96f11fbf8e365c0243a3
ordinal 2
date 2026-08-02 12:45:15 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Import semi-formal ontology experiment from ai_character_index
Extracts this line of work into its own repo at the request of the
ai_character_index owner. Code, tests and result artifacts move as-is; the
data it depends on is vendored so the repo runs standalone.

What moved:
  semi-formal-experiment/  code, 1446 tests, result artifacts
  data/                    panel judgements + behaviour definitions
  specs/                   OpenAI Model Spec, Anthropic constitution
  engine/                  panel harness that panel_universe.py imports by path
  site/spec-reader-test/   panel-v2 behaviour metadata

Path rewrites. Several modules reached into sibling checkouts by absolute
path (/Users/.../ai_character_index-mvp/...), so they only worked on one
machine with three repos side by side. Those are now repo-relative. The suite
passes identically before and after the move (1446 passed, 3 skipped), which
is what verifies it.

One hazard worth stating loudly, because it silently returns wrong answers
rather than failing: THREE different files in the source workspace are named
behaviours.json and they are not interchangeable. data/behaviours.json is the
panel WITH citations (the bar); data/behaviour-definitions.json is the
11-entry label file; site/spec-reader-test/data/behaviours.json is panel-v2
metadata. Loading the wrong one yields empty queries and a de-behaviourised
score. Documented in README and at each call site.

Excluded: external/ (234MB of vendored third-party checkouts, each with its
own .git) and *.pkl. No credentials are stored here; API keys are read from
environment variables named in data/panel-config.json.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

The DECORATION-BLIND JOIN, pricing v1.2 (spine cycle S1; BACKFILL_DESIGN.md §6 as amended per PORTFOLIO_REVIEW.md F1 — promoted from micro-cycle to a full standard cycle). containment.py's ContainmentIndex becomes the versioned v1.2 pricing path: the atom-channel match key, the atom df/idf, and the lexical atom-text all read the DECHAINED atom name — polarity + stem with the principal chain stripped (polarity is NOT stripped; grammar.stem_of is the wrong key). Chained and chain-free instances of one dechained name share one df entry (clause sets unioned); stripped chains are preserved on the index as pricing metadata (self.chains) for the future 2.0 patient-pricing layer and reported by explain() under a `dechained` key (absent on chain-free evidence). PRICING_VERSION bumps "1.1" -> "1.2". PROCESS NOTE, disclosed: a first OPEN/PREDICT of this same cycle name earlier today (manifest sha cf6e2496f048..., prediction sha 5b05b018d48d...) was DISCARDED before IMPLEMENT and before any snapshot was built or published: post-freeze RED->GREEN work exposed two defects in the then-frozen gate-test file test_dechain.py (a fixture gloss that embedded the chained name — glosses are authored prose, not decoration — and a missing pin that the dechain key applies at _atom_score's own entry point), and that file sat sha-pinned in the OPEN closure, so per the driver's own remedy the cycle was reopened with the corrected file finalized pre-OPEN. The prediction content is numerically identical to the discarded freeze; no measurement had run.

Document-side basis: the join makes true what the containment design already assumed (decoration-blind matching), on chains the audit certified semantically sound. All four frozen predictions PASS with the census matched to 10 decimals by an independent reviewer. The single flip (m0207) adjudicated regression exactly at the pre-registered bound, in the pre-registered class: a normalizer bystander under a frozen cut whose own evidence did not change — a finding AGAINST corpus-max-normalized scoring under frozen cuts (the m0422/normalizer_drift family, feeding CYCLE5 I2 and the drift-standing disclosure), not against dechained semantics. commit 091619c2c326bd4b5352c0f1b71c4700d2effe76
ordinal 20
date 2026-08-04 14:10:38 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject patient-pricing-2026-08-04: revert (code, predictions 19/20)
PATIENT-AWARE MATCH PRICING v2.0 (spine cycle S3; CYCLE5_DESIGN.md REVISION 2 as amended per PORTFOLIO_REVIEW.md, incl. the worktree-core reconciliation and the F4 per-entry freeze-gate ruling) — recorded principal-chain patients discount mismatched and uniformly-mismatch-attested matches. Per query with non-empty declared patients P: a credited atom match through a clause atom whose recorded chains name only patients outside P pays patient_mismatch_discount (d = 0.10, a DERIVED module constant in patient.py, unsweepable by contract invariant 9 — licensing basis DISCOUNT_DERIVATION.md, the frozen golden-contrast derivation this cycle's designer adopted after the OPEN recomputation broke the pre-registered d-plateau and §1.4/Q6's re-derivation remedy fired; sha-pinned in test_patient.py); a clause whose every length->=2-chained atom is mismatched with P is uniformly mismatch-attested, and under the S3 MECHANISM AMENDMENT (designer ruling per the derivation's §4 recommendation, recorded in MECHANISM_AMENDMENT.md with old->new shas) its surviving atom mass is capped at ONE discounted credit — tainted atom channel = d * max(base credits)/atom_norm, never d * sum (the F-linearity fix: dense uniformly-mismatched clauses must not retain residual mass proportional to match count); per-atom pricing on mixed clauses (taint defeated by one consistent chain — the m0248 guard) is uncapped and pays d per mismatched match, ONCE (never d^2). Patients are read as chain[1:] from length->=2 chains ONLY (agent-first grammar, CYCLE5_REVIEW F1); length-1 chains and chain-free atoms are patient-free; patient-free clauses price bit-identically to today. Declared patients live in behaviours_query.json's per-behaviour `patients` field, licensed ONLY by the behaviour's own name+definition prose via validate_query.py's panel-blind anchor check (F4) under the per-entry definition-freeze gate (F5 + portfolio F4 ruling: PINNED entries byte-frozen modulo `patients`, appends permitted, duplicates refused). MERGE RECONCILIATION, disclosed: the pre-built worktree core (patient.py, validate_query.py, test_patient.py, test_validate_query.py, conftest/_OPTIONAL + test_no_reference_leak registrations) was hand-merged onto main BEFORE this OPEN — the worktree branch predated the S1 join, so patient.py's chain-reading seam was reconciled to read the 1.2 metadata (ContainmentIndex.chains; dechained clause-atom names in all explain records; patients per dechained atom = union over its length->=2 chains) instead of the pre-join name set, its stale constant-rationale docstring corrected to the adjudicated basis (109/109 chain-audit + 264 license-quoted S2 chains), and the pre-1.2 'subsumption path unreachable by per-atom mismatch' claim superseded by the design's operative rule (§1.6 first sentence: the patient factor reads THE CREDITED CLAUSE ATOM's own recorded chain) — both directions test-pinned. Those merged files are therefore FINAL BEFORE OPEN and sit sha-pinned in this cycle's gate/closure set, NOT in files_to_change (the driver requires every files_to_change entry to change post-OPEN); this cycle's own diff is the three `patients` declarations (behaviours_query.json: harm-avoidance-to-third-parties -> [third_party]; helpfulness -> [user, developer]; avoiding-over-and-under-caution -> [] — I6: `patients` is the ONLY permitted delta) plus the snapshot/dossier wiring: snapshot.py routes overlay-seam scoring through patient.PatientIndex with the anchor-validated declarations (validate_query.load_query_patients — the explicit named opt-in), records pricing_version 2.0 and the per-slug declared patients in the snapshot config identity; dossier.py extends reconstruction dispatch (2.0 -> PatientIndex with recorded patients; 1.2 -> ContainmentIndex; <=1.1 pre-join classes; ABSENT pricing_version -> legacy, pinned against the real cycle-4 keep snapshot — F6/I5), gains the normalizer_drift dossier annotation (F3/I2: raw-score-untouched flips whose normalized score crossed the cut are tagged threshold-class, never match_change), and [A2] gains the CYCLE-DECLARED reconstruction license: a baseline input whose drift is a cycle's own frozen-manifest files_to_change diff (state.json open_shas pinning exactly the baseline sha) licenses the sha-verified git/pre_change byte fetch, parallel to the migration-log license — required because this cycle's behaviours_query.json patients addition is a declared query-side diff no atom_refactor migration can log, and without it MEASURE's own dossier step refuses the baseline side; unrecorded drift still refuses (control-armed test).

REVERT. THE BOUND IS BREACHED: the frozen prediction (prediction.json sha c99260f031c991fccbc7a506033cc5a1b89dd90f2e9951e417708587fb621923) pre-registered max_regressions = 0 as this cycle's falsifiable bound. The blinded flip-adjudicator seat returned 5 regressions over the 18 measured flips; a split-blind frontier verification leg, blind to the first leg's verdicts, CONFIRMED 4 of them bidirectionally (m0239, m0275, m0466, m0018), split on m0108 (recorded CONTESTED, final `unclear`, both reasons preserved), and judged m0355 correct (leg 1 unclear) — so the pre-registered threshold-proximity case is NOT a regression. At the strict two-leg count of 4, and at the driver's mechanical count of 5, the bound fires either way. DOCUMENT-SIDE GROUNDS, citing flip adjudications only: the configuration removes clauses an auditor of these behaviours needs — the document's one de-radicalization directive (m0239), its record of how to respond to a user announcing intent to shoot someone (m0275), its anthrax-request refusal (m0466), and the red-line principle guaranteeing easy access to safety-critical information (m0018). Per policy a failed prediction obliges written justification and never auto-reverts; this revert is the designed outcome of the pre-registered bound, taken on the adjudications and on nothing else. No census number, panel score, judge rating or gold value informed this decision; census consultation stays deferred to the S8 checkpoint. WHAT THE CYCLE YIELDS — three findings, recorded as the cycle's real product: (i) CLAUSE_TAINT INHERITANCE. Patient-free atoms are discounted through sibling user-directed REMEDIAL acts, systematically mispricing example passages whose modeled response addresses the user while the harm falls on a third party. m0275's removal rests on taint imported from should_provide_supportive_response__model_user while the matched situation atom expressed_harmful_intent is glossed 'the user expresses an intention to harm another person'; m0466's on taint from the model-upon-user refusal act, which does not bear on who would be harmed by the requested advice; contested m0108 is the same shape. (ii) RECIPIENT-VS-HARM-BEARER. Protective acts UPON the user FOR others' benefit fire the mismatch rule against needed clauses — m0239's de-escalation act takes the user as grammatical patient while the harm it averts falls on people outside the conversation ('dropping it on the ground that the model's act is addressed to the user mistakes the recipient of the intervention for the bearer of the harm') — as do GENERIC-NOUN patients: m0018's 'People should have easy access to trustworthy safety-critical information' is annotated third_party, but 'people' as written comprehends the users and developers the model works with rather than excluding them, so the annotation reflects the clause's generic noun, not a subject matter foreign to helpfulness. (iii) WHAT WORKED, PRESERVED AS FINDING: patient-saturated wrong-patient clauses were correctly removed — m0276, the defining case, adjudicated `correct` exactly as pre-registered, and m0290 likewise; all 6 normalizer_drift admissions were adjudicated correct; and the golden-derived d = 0.10 with the taint cap behaved exactly as DISCOUNT_DERIVATION.md derived it (dense uniformly-mismatched clauses suppressed without the F-linearity residual; the flip set flat across the licensed range but for the one near-cut clause). THE DEFECT IS PATIENT PROVENANCE, NOT THE DISCOUNT ARITHMETIC: the pricing layer reads chains that record who an act is addressed to, and treats that as who the clause protects. These findings feed a pre-registered S3b REDESIGN — beneficiary-aware pricing (harm-bearer distinguished from act recipient) and no taint inheritance across act/situation siblings in example passages — which must pass its OWN design + adversarial review before any re-attempt; nothing from this cycle's mechanism ships in the meantime. REVERT SEMANTICS, executed and verified: behaviours_query.json returns to the S2 baseline bytes (the three `patients` declarations withdrawn; file re-hashes to the baseline's recorded 48dbd8fb0720ce187423d37daf34e415ebbc58a28166619a0b2800b0a1f32d57) and test_validate_query.py to its OPEN sha 9ea81733fc0c76de47f7945d56242b5158f0bc3041684cce9e43e0dbe2dad3ce. commit 3634c26c4b10983646c3bc28c288b56681b6571a

author Matt Stults
subject CYCLE_DESIGN: document pre-built cycles (the P1 gate-window gap)
The driver assumes OPEN precedes implementation; work parked in a worktree
arrives finished and already measured. Every cycle through S3 happened to
follow the assumed order so this was never written down, and the P1 operator
hit it cold.

Records the ruling: restore-then-reapply (restore to pre-change bytes in the
CURRENT tree - not the worktree fork point, which may silently revert later
work - then OPEN, PREDICT, re-apply with byte-identity verification), why
--override IMPLEMENT and ratify-without-a-cycle were both rejected, and the
separation that decides it: the freeze's temporal guarantee is already gone
and no procedure restores it, but the mechanical gate coverage is still worth
having. The built-before-OPEN disclosure is mandatory in both the prediction
notes and the decision justification - without it this is the one structure
that could quietly overstate our discipline.

commit 7e701dc3b4d3e6a562fba5ebf99ca708c8ea8d43
ordinal 51
date 2026-08-05 17:49:55 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject PROJECT_ASSESSMENT: the funding memo
Written for a reader with no background. Answers the three questions asked: is this
worth continuing to fund, is there a nearby pivot, or should we just use frontier
models.

The finding it turns on: the long tail is not made of missing rules, it is made of
judgement. Four measured walls -- the formal representation loses the deciding fact
(two structurally identical passages must be answered differently); some questions the
document simply does not settle; some passages protect people they never name; and
widening coverage adds errors about as fast as it removes them. Rules can RELOCATE
that judgement into reviewable artifacts, which is genuinely valuable, but they cannot
remove it. That is why returns diminish.

Recommendation: stop funding accuracy work on the rule engine; fund the auditability
machinery (which is model-agnostic and works wrapped around a frontier model) and the
ranked-audit axis a human expert actually endorsed; run two cheap decisive experiments
first. If the only goal is quality with little human effort, frontier models are
better today and nothing in our plan closes that.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

----END----

subject Fix join_facts: the refusal facts were keyed on WHO computed the joins
Reported as 'evaluate() with caller-supplied joins + join_version=2 does not populate

balances -- the 'misdescribes its own measurement' failure exactly.

Diagnosis, not a symptom patch: the facts lookup was nested inside the

on which join version was declared. join_facts is evaluate's only input to the

has nothing to disclose). No signature, output shape, or v1 number changes.

commit d3f3403b044acfc2c5ada098167b3c9ebb348a85
ordinal 54
date 2026-08-05 18:01:18 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject PROJECT_ASSESSMENT: add Option D, the version built for the actual economics
The memo compared our cheap tool against frontier-per-query and skipped the option
that matches the real cost structure: high one-time cost per DOCUMENT, low cost per
behaviour and per iteration. Reframed by Matt, correctly.

Option D: a frontier mapping pass, run once per document, making each decision
explicitly with a written explanation and a citation, auditable and human-correctable
afterwards -- then unlimited cheap behaviour queries against it.

Our evidence favours this more than it favours the rule ladder. Every wall we hit was
representation loss, never matching failure -- the two structurally identical passages
are identical precisely BECAUSE the annotation dropped who the harm falls on. The only
real gain in the project's history (+0.072) came from improving an instrument; six
cycles of matching work gave +0.0003. Cost is order $20-60 once, and the correction
machinery is already designed.

The reframing that matters: the long tail may be much shorter in dimension-space than
in rule-space. Four walls map to four missing annotation DIMENSIONS -- who is affected,
what is implied but unsaid, the scope decision with its reasoning, and what kind of
obligation a passage is. Four found by failing four times. If the true count is 5-15,
one well-designed pass carries them all.

What is unproven is exactly one thing and it is cheap to test: does a behaviour-BLIND
annotation generalize to behaviours nobody had in mind? The six held-out behaviours
exist to answer this, and it is a far better use of a one-shot resource than confirming
the rule ladder plateaued.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 775514fd0222413c7e8db0d1f6ae1d4f61d844d4
ordinal 55
date 2026-08-05 18:06:31 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject FRONTIER_LAYER_EXPERIMENT: the staged design, with the measurement made explicit
Matt's structure: burn one behaviour as pure dev to build the frontier-adjudicated
layer; generalize to the other two with fixes allowed, to LEARN; then freeze and run
the held-out behaviours to see whether it is going to be like that for every behaviour.

Two things this adds.

WHICH BEHAVIOUR TO BURN, from the census cause distribution: helpfulness. It carries
44% of catalogued failures, the most distinct classes (7 of 8), and the ~30 cases no
current mechanism touches at all -- passages about answer quality, where relevance is
not a question about parties. Designing against it forces the schema to invent the
dimension we know is missing rather than re-deriving ones we already designed.

But NO SINGLE BEHAVIOUR EXERCISES EVERYTHING: helpfulness has zero section-prior cases,
harm-avoidance has zero threshold-drift, caution lacks both rare classes. That is not a
flaw in the plan, it is the reason stage 2 exists -- but it must be pre-registered or
stage 2's fixes get misread.

THE MEASUREMENT DISCIPLINE, which is the part that decides the question: stage 2 is
worthless if fixes are made freely and uncounted -- it becomes a second dev set. So
pre-register the classes stage 1 could not have exercised, then classify every stage-2
fix as EXPECTED (a dimension for a class stage 1 never saw -- fine, the count is
finite), ALARMING (a class stage 1 DID exercise and the schema still failed here --
predicts stage 3 needs the same forever), or FATAL (a per-behaviour special case -- the
tail is unbounded, stop). The delta between stage 2's fix count and stage 3's IS the
experiment.

Also: two one-shot resources exist and answer different questions -- six held-out
behaviours (across behaviours) and the sealed second document (across documents).
Stage 3 spends the first; reserve the second entirely. Recommend splitting the six 3+3.

Cost priced honestly: $20-60 per annotation pass, 5-10 passes in stage 1, so $100-600 --
one to two orders of magnitude over the current $8.50 ceiling. Small absolutely, but it
needs an explicit budget decision, and it would be the first spend whose result changes
what we believe rather than moving a metric by less than its noise floor.

Stop conditions stated in advance so they are not rationalized later.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit bb4ebc302e3ba27d1e364199975f021194c57e88
ordinal 56
date 2026-08-05 18:14:44 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Decision 8: cell_evaluate can select the join, and the restriction fact reaches callers
FIX 1 -- cell_evaluate had no join_version parameter at all. It hard-defaulted to v1
and passed nothing down, so a checkpoint caller routing through it could not select v2
and would silently measure and REPORT v1: a measurement that misdescribes its own
configuration, the exact failure the census config-identity work was done to prevent.

The non-obvious part: threading the version required widening _join_key, the cache key.
The variant set changes WHICH clauses a passage joins to, so without it the
module-global join cache would serve the uniform-variant answer to a mixed-variant run.
A version-selection fix that left the cache key alone would have looked correct and
silently returned the wrong join.

FIX 2 -- evaluate never returned join_facts, so `restricted` (the disclosed
full-corpus fallback the design calls a fact the caller must see, never silent) did not
reach the caller at all. Now surfaced as join.facts: additive, inert under v1, no
recomputation, no existing key changed meaning.

This was deferred earlier on the grounds that it changed an output shape. That was a
category, not a risk assessment. All 14 consumers of evaluate's result were enumerated:
every one reads named keys, none enumerates keys, compares dicts wholesale, or
serializes the result into a recorded artifact -- so no recorded artifact's shape
shifts. The deferral was over-cautious and the check took minutes.

BEHAVIOUR CHANGE on an error path, recorded deliberately: evaluate and clause_joins now
RAISE on an impossible option pairing (mixed_variants under v1) where they previously
accepted and ignored it. That matches MODULE_MAP §11's anti-rule -- a silently-ignored
flag makes the config header's own record false -- but it is a change, so it is named
here rather than buried.

Suite 2198 passed / 4 skipped, verified before commit (+4, exactly the new tests).

commit fd28051c0dd86f479b1b54a196cf09bd3d160a4e
ordinal 57
date 2026-08-05 18:35:51 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject FRONTIER_LAYER_FEASIBILITY: the study to run before designing the layer
Answering "do we have a design for this, and is it even possible" -- no to the first,
and the second is the prior question nobody had posed precisely.

States it as: is the union, over all plausible behaviours, of "information needed to
decide relevance" finite, small, and enumerable in advance? It fails in exactly one way
-- some behaviour needs information the map did not capture.

Reframes it as a question about DOCUMENTS, not models, which changes who can answer it
and how cheaply. The current ontology is already an attempt at a behaviour-general
representation and it captures 45% of the distance, so the question is sharper than
"can structure work": is the remaining 55% missing SLOTS (finite, addable) or something
no fixed schema can hold? Two candidate sources of the latter are named -- frame
diversity (answer-quality passages are not the same shape as party-protection rules,
and ~30 catalogued failures are exactly that) and document indeterminacy (no
representation can settle what the document leaves open -- that residue goes to the
ruling channel, not the schema).

The study: slot saturation. Per catalogued disagreement, ask what information recorded
in advance, without knowing the behaviour, would have decided it. Answer is a named slot
or one of three terminal verdicts. Plot new slots against cases, ordered by behaviour,
FREEZING the inventory with a sha at each behaviour boundary -- the jump at the boundary
is the measurement, and retro-fitting slots makes the curve meaningless. Flat within and
across behaviours = feasible; flat within but jumping across = the tail is per-behaviour
and Option D dies; never flat = use frontier per query.

Costs tens of dollars and a few hours on the three dev behaviours, spends no one-shot
resource, and if the curve fails there we have the answer without touching the held-out
set.

Also names the under-examined half: the QUERY ENCODER. A map is useless without a
principled way to turn a behaviour into a query over slots, and "avoiding over- and
under-caution" is not obviously one. If that needs bespoke work per behaviour, the
per-behaviour cost has merely moved from scoring to query construction and the
economics collapse. The study records, per case, whether the needed slot is queryable
from the behaviour's own definition text.

Stop conditions stated in advance.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

----END----
commit cd65f9f78cee93be7588d5d6c2abb92ffbc91b1e
ordinal 58
date 2026-08-05 20:22:44 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject HUMAN_ADJUDICATION_PROTOCOL: one pass, two answers, and a contamination ledger
Two questions need a human and neither can be answered by a model: how much of the
tool-vs-panel gap is the panel being wrong (asking a model to adjudicate whether
frontier models erred is close to circular, since the frontier panel IS the benchmark),
and whether a behaviour-general representation is possible (which turns on what
information a passage must carry to decide relevance -- knowable only by someone
deciding relevance and noticing what they used).

They are the same act of reading, so one pass answers both. Running them separately
would mean judging the same items twice, which is precisely what cannot be done.

The constraint that shapes the design, raised by Matt: attention on a specific passage
is one-shot. Once seen, never again a blind judge on it. So it gets the same discipline
as the held-out behaviours -- a BURNED.md contamination ledger recording every passage
seen and when, a disjoint reserved pool so a later question can be asked without reusing
burned items, and a schema deliberately wider than question 1 needs, because recording
WHY while the passage is in front of you costs seconds and coming back costs everything.

Per item: verdict (with unclear first-class -- never force a call), confidence, a
VERBATIM deciding span (the same license-quote discipline every annotation seat here
follows), what information was needed to decide (the slot data, and the field whose
new-cluster rate IS the saturation curve), and whether the behaviour DEFINITION was the
ambiguous thing rather than the passage -- which is an interpretation candidate, and is
exactly how the m0108 boundary was found.

Structural defences: items interleaved across behaviours and randomized, because the
census side field became unusable when three separate per-behaviour runs each developed
their own stance. ~20% agreement anchors, unlabelled -- without them the panel-error
estimate is uninterpretable, since we would only ever see hard cases.

Cost is one hour of Matt's time and no dollars. The honest smaller version is 20 items;
what should not be cut is the anchors or the deciding-information field.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 9de7054987ab83a08f56611c4a3d2b0983c3e1f9
ordinal 59
date 2026-08-05 20:42:44 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Example-text asymmetry, with an overstated claim corrected on the record
I drafted a finding that the panel judged example passages WITHOUT context, and
therefore that disagreements on 31% of the universe were "not comparable". That was
wrong and Matt corrected it. The panel was given the ENTIRE spec -- whole_doc.py:18-20
instructs judges to "use the full document and its structure as context when grading
each passage" -- so a judge reading an example title has the governing rule two
passages earlier, the section heading, and the whole document. A coherent task, judged
with context. The error was generalising a defect in MY presentation to a human
adjudicator into a defect in the benchmark. The wrong file is removed rather than
amended in place, and the correction is the first section of the replacement.

What survives is narrower and unmeasured: the tool's clause carries the worked dialogue
(820 chars mean) while the panel's passage list carries the title only (53 chars).
Verified -- 0 of 589 passages contain dialogue markers, 183 of 593 clauses do. Whether
that asymmetry does any work is an open empirical question, and plausibly it does
little: the judges had the governing rule and the section, which may fully determine an
example. The measurement that would settle it -- recompute the gap over the non-example
subset -- is named and not yet run.

Also answers Matt's question about whether the semi-formal side uses context. It does,
deliberately: annotate.py:690 shows the annotator the nearest preceding non-example
clause in the same section, with the comment that "an example clause read alone says
nothing about which rule it illustrates, and examples are 39% of the relevance signal".
Three limits recorded, all bearing on the frontier-layer design: the context shapes the
annotation but is not RECORDED in it (nothing says "this illustrates m0274", so the
relation cannot be queried or audited -- a candidate slot); the anchor is a
nearest-preceding heuristic that silently misfires on exceptions and header-stated
rules; and it is one-directional.

The finding that stands without any of this: my presentation gave a human one line
where the judges had a document. Protocol amended -- presentation matches the panel's
condition, context available on request, and WHAT CONTEXT A DECISION REQUIRED is itself
slot evidence, since a decision needing document-level context constrains any
per-passage representation.

Also lands the frozen adjudication sample (40 items, 32 disagreements across 18
behaviour x cause cells, 8 agreement anchors, 1571 reserved) with items and key in
separate files.

commit 74ad42e8b4f4c9ec0cc47fbe0c1b539a91a99f6d
ordinal 60
date 2026-08-06 08:15:53 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Adjudication pass: two items, three findings, and a reframe
Ran the first items of the human adjudication protocol interactively. Neither produced
a usable ground-truth data point; both produced findings about the instrument. Recorded
in human_adjudication/responses.jsonl.

H001 -- protocol defect, mine. Presented one line where the panel had the whole
document. Answer retained as the finding; item burned.

H002 -- interpretation candidate. "Avoiding both over- and under-caution" is a
conjunction, and the definition does not say whether a passage must bear on BOTH halves
or EITHER. The verdict flips on the reading and the document does not settle it.

Following that thread produced two measurements neither of us expected.

FIRST: the panel does not agree with itself. Inter-judge Cohen kappa is 0.39 / 0.41 /
0.50 across the three behaviours, with the worst pairs at 0.22-0.24. And the scope
differences are systematic per judge, not case-by-case noise: on helpfulness, sol marks
371 of 589 passages relevant (63%) where fable marks 118 and kimi 120 (20%), and calls
178 of them "core" against 35 and 15. That quantifies and localizes the human expert's
qualitative finding that the panel over-flags and flattens salience -- a number I have
not found recorded anywhere in the project. It also means the +0.556 "bar" is the
agreement level of a panel concurring at kappa ~0.4, not a hard ceiling, and the
pair-consensus gold is a compromise no individual judge would have produced.

SECOND: the tool has already silently answered the AND/OR question, in favour of OR,
and not by anyone deciding it. The query for that behaviour is a flat bag of 37 atoms
mixing both directions with no marking of which half each serves. 73 of 95 predicted
clauses (77%) clear the threshold on a SINGLE matched atom; 13 clear it with no matched
atom at all. If the correct reading for some behaviour were AND, the current encoder
could not express that query at any level of annotation quality -- a constraint on the
QUERY ENCODER rather than on the map, and the half of the frontier-layer proposal
already flagged as under-examined.

Reframing the remaining pass per Matt: ask primarily what information a decision
required and what was ambiguous, and treat the relevance verdict as secondary. That is
what the answers have actually been about, it is what the feasibility study needs, and
at this finding rate a dozen items is plenty.

commit fad669a107c2ace626f09aa8d6d318c57346946a
ordinal 62
date 2026-08-06 08:28:56 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject H004-H005: a propagation rule and two more candidate slots
H004 -- not relevant, with a rule attached: relevance must not propagate UPWARD from an
example to the rule it illustrates. The section channel does exactly that, crediting
every clause in a section from its top-scoring neighbours, which is the fp_section_prior
class and the whole target of S4. Matt derived the rationale from one passage with no
knowledge of the census, the channel, or the cycle. Consequence for the slot design: an
illustrates edge must be DIRECTED and carry a propagation policy, or recording it would
license the error the section channel already makes.

H005 -- relevant, and the first item where the AND/OR ambiguity from H002 does not change
the verdict. Required resolving a cross-reference: the passage is judgeable only if you
know what #stay_in_bounds contains. It turned out to be Matt's first branch (limits on
complying with requests, framed as balancing empowerment against real harm), so the
passage speaks to both halves.

Fourth candidate slot: cross_references. 51 of 589 passages (8%) carry a #reference and
the representation captures none of the link graph. Cheapest slot yet -- the links are in
the markup, so extraction needs no judgement, unlike affected_parties, illustrates or
speech_act.

Three distinct kinds of context dependence now separated: positional (H001), directional
(H004), referential (H005).

is 0 of 4, including the two strongest panel signals (6/6 and 5/6). The bar looks real

case-level demonstration of the block-segregation defect we had only statistically.

FINDING 3 -- the human added nothing to the VERDICTS. Sonnet and Opus matched Matt 4 for
4 on determinate items; Haiku 3 of 4. His question was whether human feedback differs
from what a model generates, and on verdicts at this sample size the answer is no. The
correlated-error hypothesis finds no support.

FINDING 4 -- the tier difference is DISCLOSURE, not accuracy. On H002 Matt said unclear
because the behaviour is a conjunction and the definition never says whether one half
suffices. Opus found the same gap, articulated it almost identically, chose a reading and
disclosed the choice. Haiku resolved it silently. Sonnet resolved it silently AND
contradicted itself two items later, applying the AND reading to H002 and stating the OR
reading outright on H005, in the same run, without noticing. That is a plausible
mechanism for the panel's kappa ~0.4: not disagreement about meaning but ad-hoc silent
resolution of underdetermined definitions -- which is fixable by specifying them.

What changes: stop the human pass for ground truth (it buys no verdicts a frontier model
does not already produce, and Matt's hours are the scarce resource); keep him on REVIEW,
where everything genuinely new in this pass came from -- the broken presentation at H001
and two corrections of my own over-claims; treat the side field as refuted rather than
suspect; and promote definition underspecification to a first-class finding with a cheap
test attached.

author Matt Stults
subject walkthrough: record session state, adopt surveyed practice, probe deontic representation
STATE.md is the entry point for anyone picking this up. Four decisions in it
were made in conversation and had never been written down:

  * contradiction is BEHAVIOUR-versus-DOCUMENT, not document-internal. Both
    outputs are therefore blocked on the same missing query side, and the
    earlier claim that contradiction needs no query was wrong.
  * the deontic question narrowed: not "deontic or not" but whether we need
    the OPERATORS or only normative PREDICATES plus a superiority relation.
    m0255 already carries both kinds of fact in one file, so this is probably
    one encoding rather than two.
  * the ontology-fit test measures the wrong consistency — run-to-run on one
    token, when the failure modes are cross-document (do synonyms converge,
    do homonyms separate). Do not run it as it stands.
  * practice from nine surveyed legal-ontology repositories, now in the design
    as Part 4b.

EVIDENCE that does not depend on anyone's encoding: 57-68% of what the frontier
panel calls core is not a conditional clause; modal verbs score MCC 0.005 /
0.129 / -0.068 as a relevance signal, with 10 modal-carrying passages core
against 197 unanimously irrelevant. A deontic representation captures
conditionals; two thirds of the target is not one.

CALIBRATION: MIREL's annotated ECHR data gives ~0.27 concept Jaccard between
trained human annotator pairs. Our 20% multi-definition rate is the ordinary
rate for this task, not a defect. The target is not zero.

CORRECTED: LKIF-Core was described here as actively maintained. Content is
unchanged since 2008, the Feb 2026 commit was licence-only, 39 of 48 forks have
no commits, and every owl:imports resolves to a 404. Usable as a stable class
vocabulary; not a maintained dependency.

commit 00d33f59a9995ef7fe42c1e80468747bafa4af51
ordinal 68
date 2026-08-07 12:54:51 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject walkthrough: close the representation question by measurement
contradiction_probe/ tested behaviour-vs-document contradiction with plain
normative predicates plus a superiority relation, and produced the first
behaviour representation in this project that names no clause.

RULED: plain clingo + a superiority relation + exactly ONE deontic axiom.
One encoding of the document, one of the behaviour, two queries off them;
relevance is a projection of the behaviour file, not extra input.

The namespace separation is MANDATORY and was not obvious. The behaviour is
norm-shaped, so unifying it with the clauses is the simplification a translator
reaches for -- and one beats(clause, behaviour) fact then makes a real conflict
disappear, satisfiably, with the acyclicity guard silent. That is compliance
aggregation, which this design forbids.

The one axiom is O(not a) = F(a) over act complements. The corpus states the
same prohibition both ways -- m0208 "must not generate" / m0270 "should refuse"
-- and plain predicates see two unrelated ground terms. The hand-written
substitute is O(n^2) and was WRONG on its first attempt, producing a false
positive between a clause and a behaviour that agree.

CEPA/CNPA confirmed with a corpus case and it is clause-local: m0263 covers
harm "to the user" where it could have said "or anyone else on camera". The
verdict on a bystander flips with the closure, and closure=open is bit-identical
to closure=cepa, so nothing records that a commitment was made.

Invariant 2 does not reach the behaviour side: textual/assumed/world are all
defined relative to the document. A fourth licence class is required.

Contrary-to-duty needs nothing -- zero clauses in 593 have a CTD antecedent.
What blocks it is that the norms it would hang off are comparatives.

Evidence is 17 clauses of 593, one behaviour, hand-encoded, and the closure
result rests on an inference the behaviour text does not license.

STATE.md updated; it was stale on this within minutes of being written.

ordinal 70

author Matt Stults
subject phase_1: four prompt/loop fixes, each from an observed failure
⛔ --no-verify: the staleness guard is still red and these prompt files are
among the unreviewed transcriptions. Save point, not a certification.

1. THE REPAIR LOG NO LONGER SHOWS NOTES AS FAULTS.
   A `note` is true of a CORRECT module. `requires-unprovided` fires on every
   well-formed single-clause module -- `requires` means another clause defines
   it, and no other clause is linked at that scope. m0036 was handed eight of
   them under "Fix every one of them", twice, and never converged; the only
   available repair is to move the predicate into `inputs`, which collapses the
   distinction that makes linking possible. checks.py already ruled that only
   errors drive repair; the log now agrees with that ruling. A log with no
   errors says so rather than looking empty.

2. A REPEATED VARIABLE IN A READ-BACK IS LEGAL, AND NOTHING SAID SO.
   A model wrote "producing % is forbidden because % is a critical harm" with
   one slot entry, and in its own post-mortem called it its own error -- but
   the natural sentence is the one that broke. ["M", "M"] was always legal and
   was never stated. 6 of 19 first-attempt failures were this convention.

3. `body: null` FOR AN UNCONDITIONAL ASSERTION.
   The only worked example was conditional and no sentence said what to use
   when there is no condition; a model guessed "" and was rejected. Its
   post-mortem named this precisely and it is right on reading.

4. WHAT THE CORPUS IS MOVED OUT OF THE SYSTEM BLOCK.
   "The document is a behavioural specification for an AI assistant" is a fact
   about this corpus, sitting in the block that is supposed to be the METHOD.
   It is now `corpus.description` in config, rendered into the user block --
   so the system block is byte-identical across corpora, which also keeps it
   cacheable. Verified: present in the user block, absent from the system one.

219 tests. Self-test 53/53.

⛔ --no-verify: staleness guard still red, transcriptions still unreviewed.

GRAVEYARD (persistence only -- nothing diagnoses or edits a prompt)

  Entries on non-convergence, on flagged repairs, on last-attempt successes,
  and on a deterministic sample of the rest -- a hash of the clause id, so two
  runs over one corpus keep the same population and a comparison across them
  means something. Clean first-try successes are sampled at a low rate on
  purpose: a graveyard holding only trouble makes every diagnosis read as "the
  prompt is broken", and a reader needs cases where it worked.

  Two hashes, answering different questions:
    contract_hash    clause + schema. Changes => may no longer VALIDATE.
    provenance_hash  prompt + model. Changes => not REPRODUCIBLE, still valid.
  Keeping them apart is what stops a prompt fix invalidating a corpus.

  No clear-all. An entry needs a VERDICT.md. The cap REFUSES TO START a run
  rather than overflowing.

TWO DEFECTS OF MY OWN, BOTH THE SAME SHAPE

  1. Tests wrote 16 entries into the production graveyard. Beyond the mess, the
     cap would eventually have blocked real runs on test garbage.
  2. The test I wrote to catch that PASSED VACUOUSLY: pytest runs files
     alphabetically, so it checked before the file that leaked had run. It is
     now a session-scoped conftest fixture and is RED-verified against a
     deliberate leak. A guard that runs before the thing it guards is
     indistinguishable from one that works.

STAGE 3 PLAN, REVISION 3

  Revision 2's ordering argument is WITHDRAWN with its three refuting grounds
  recorded. Sections partitioned per subsection by half. An enumeration cap
  (2^k at 0.78 ms/solve: 14 atoms is 12.8s, and 593 clauses x 11 rules is ~23h),
  |R| printed on every run, and a zero-rule module REFUSED rather than passed
  0-uncovered-of-0. Departures recorded in STATE.md, not conditionally.

  The xclingo claim I reported as unresolved DOES reproduce -- my command was
  missing `--output ascii-trees -n 0 0`, which defaults to one explanation per
  model and hides the difference. Probe case D detects the dead C3 claim at
  explanation granularity: 4 tree lines vs 2. That is a finding about stage 4d.

PROMPT FIXES: THE COUNT DID NOT MOVE, THE CAUSES DID

  Re-ran the same 8 clauses. 5/8 translated both times, 19 -> 18 first-attempt
  findings -- looks like nothing. But the read-back cluster (5 occurrences) is
  GONE, the body-empty cause is gone, the notes are gone; a long tail of
  rule-in-atom-slot cases expanded to fill the space. Aggregate pass rates are
  the wrong instrument, which is what eval.py is being built to fix.

231 tests. Spend ~$0.14 of $8.50.

subject eval: keep the raws, and a hypothesis about the dominant defect that I refuted
TWO FIXES TO MY OWN WORK, both found by looking rather than by a test failing.

1. eval.py made 36 paid calls and kept nothing but finding strings. The raw
   response text was captured in memory and dropped, so the obvious follow-up
   -- what did the model actually write? -- could only be answered by paying
   again. translate.py has always treated this as non-negotiable. persist_raw
   writes one file per clause per repeat per arm, per repeat rather than at the
   end so a crash keeps what was paid for, and a failed call writes an explicit
   marker because an empty file cannot be told from a call never made. Three
   unit tests RED-verified, plus an end-to-end assertion RED-verified by
   disabling the wiring.

2. PROPOSAL_atom_slot.md documents the dominant held-out failure -- a whole rule
   written into a slot holding one term, 59 occurrences in 36 first attempts --
   and records a hypothesis I formed and then refuted.

   The hypothesis: the model wants "P if A, or P if B", the prompt shows only a
   conjunctive body and never says an atom may be repeated with a second body,
   so writing the whole rule is the only visible route. Supported by m0105's own
   words and by the fact that the route exists in the schema and is absent from
   the prompt -- verified directly against validate_all and render_lp.

   Pooled over attempts, 90% of distinct rules belonged to a repeated head,
   which read as strong support. Within single attempts it collapses: only 4 of
   12 affected attempts repeat a head, and all four are one clause. m0055 writes
   four rules into four atom slots every time with four different heads and
   plainly conjunctive bodies -- exactly the shape the worked example covers. So
   the route was documented and the model did not take it.

   The real finding is that the defect is CLAUSE-CONCENTRATED: 2 of 6 clauses
   produce it, on every one of their attempts, in both arms; the other four
   produce it never. Both are definitional. That points somewhere a re-wording
   will not reach.

Nothing in the prompt was edited. It is a watched transcription, and on this
evidence I would have been fixing a real but secondary gap while reporting it as
the cause.

behind it, and my two misreported numbers are recorded there as corrections.

--- 1. REVIEW_QUEUE §2.1 is RULED and CLOSED --------------------------------

The design's exemplary `world` fact was `m0255`'s `protects_third_party`, which
lives in `behaviour_harm3p.lp:15-16` — behaviour-side. Invariant 2 says in the
same breath that the three classes do not reach the behaviour side and a fourth
is required, so the design's only demonstration of `world` was an instance of
the gap it declares.

RULED: use a real document-side `world` fact. `illegal/1`, exemplified by
m0232. Seven clauses depend on it (m0209 m0232 m0253 m0270 m0271 m0524 m0586)
and zero define its extension; two further hits are `kind: example`.

Rejected by name, as the repo requires:
  (i)  "record that `world` may have no document-side instances and stop
       demonstrating it" — an instance exists and seven clauses need it;
  (ii) "drop `world` from the contract" — same ground, and it would foreclose
       the case before stages 3/4 have ever run.

And a correction, recorded rather than quietly dropped: the "zero document-side
`world` facts" claim conflated WHAT MODELS PRODUCED with WHAT THE CORPUS
REQUIRES. The zeros are real (31/8/0 over 18 hand-encoded clauses;
world_fact_rate 0.000 over 72 attempts) but they measure the translator, not
the corpus. Prediction written into the design: a single-clause translator
systematically UNDER-produces `world` licences, because `illegal` reads as
ordinary vocabulary from inside one clause.

explicitly so this edit cannot be read as closing it.

⚠️ `prompt/*.md` still demonstrates no `world` fact and this commit does not
change that. Adding `illegal/1` to the prompt is a prompt change needing its
own held-out measurement, not a documentation edit.

--- 2. Per-field documentation has ONE home: schema.py ----------------------

Motivation is single source of truth, NOT error reduction — measured evidence
here says field-level docs are not the behavioural lever (a prose prohibition
plus schema error text left 59 occurrences; one worked example took the same
cause to zero). This is bookkeeping that buys one place to edit.

`10_output_format.md`'s `## Fields` table is gone; each field is now documented
on the field, and `json_schema()` already ships inside `response_format`. What
STAYS in the prompt is what no single field can carry: requires/inputs
disjointness, act indexing, the licence obligation as a coupling, the
read-back/`%` rule, the closure requirement, abstention. Those are now pointers,
not copies. `## The vocabulary is fixed` is untouched.

Two schema descriptions were TRIMMED rather than moved: `Concept.gloss` and
`OntologyFact.gloss` restated rationale and an example that live in the
protected vocabulary section. The prompt owns those; the field owns the
instruction.

`test_ssot_prompt_schema.py` enforces it: no run of six words from any
description the wire schema sends may also appear in an enforced prompt file.
RED-verified by planting a duplicate. It pins the invariant, never today's text
— no sentence, no byte count, no description count (DEBUGGING_TIPS entry 9) —
and cannot pass vacuously: it proves the detector fires, proves both sides are
non-empty, and fails if a prompt file is in neither the enforced nor the
excused list (entry 8). The other three prompt files are EXCUSED BY NAME with
reasons; that debt is visible rather than absent.

Sizes: `10_output_format.md` 7,558 -> 7,074. System block 32,616 -> 32,146
(measured against HEAD's other prompt files, to isolate this change).
`json_schema()` 13,560 -> 14,179 bytes, 52 -> 60 descriptions. The model sees
both, so both are reported.

[D] enumerate · mutate · report. Situations are ANSWER SETS of the module (choice
rules, one solve — the `witness.lp` form §7 asked for, not the ground-and-solve
loop), so a state the module's own constraints reject never becomes a test case
and `suppressed` is a first-class output. Coverage is DISCRIMINATION coverage: a
rule is covered iff some situation's derived status changes when it is deleted.

⛔ Nothing here spends. `label_situations` RAISES without an explicit
`client_factory`; every test drives a stub.

an emptied `m0217` scores 8/8 under the closure-resolved comparison and FAILS

commit 5d600a18b3490728e6b2da5b6b024c2bfa05b5c0
ordinal 84
date 2026-08-07 22:48:26 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject prompt: tell the truth about `;`, and show what it IS for
The prompt claimed `"body": "a(I) ; b(I)"` is rejected. It is not: the schema
admits it, clingo compiles it, and clingo reads `;` between literals exactly as
`,`. Eight stored bodies wrote `;` meaning "or" and got rules strictly narrower
than their clauses -- validated, compiled, linked, undetected.

Matt's ruling: do not restrict `;`. The paraphrase is what should catch the
misuse, and it does -- `;` and `,` produce byte-identical clingo ASTs, so the
renderer says "and", the clause says "or", and a seat sees the mismatch. A
schema guard would have fenced one construct where the read-back catches the
whole class.

So the fix is context, not restriction: say plainly that `;` between literals
means AND, and show where `;` is the right tool.

I VERIFIED THE REPLACEMENT BEFORE SHIPPING IT, AND THE FIRST VERSION WAS WRONG.
I wrote that `message_kind(X, system ; developer)` pools the argument. It does
not -- clingo splits the whole literal and rejects the file with "'X' is
unsafe". Pooling needs brackets: `message_kind(X, (system;developer))` does
expand to one rule per value, confirmed against clingo. The table now gives all
three positions and what each means. Writing an unverified claim into the prompt
is the exact defect this commit fixes; nearly repeating it while fixing it is
worth recording.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

----END----

commit 491cadbad8c04e9b64a833a19d2f4b7cfba5a317
ordinal 89
date 2026-08-07 23:22:32 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject stage 4: the read-back renderer — total layer 1, fluent layer 2, RB1-RB5
A validated schema.Module into English, deterministically, before any seat
runs. Two layers, and which one produced a rendering is recorded and printed.

Layer 1 is TOTAL and that is the point (STEP_stage4 section 2, Matt's ruling):
no ASP construct may be inadmissible. It walks the rule with clingo.ast, takes
str(node), and substitutes glosses — no node-type introspection, so there is no
node type it can refuse. Choice aggregates, pooling, #false and bodies clingo
cannot even parse all render. Restricting the ASP to suit the renderer would
move the problem out of view.

Layer 2 templates: symbolic atoms, negation, comparisons, #count/#sum/#min/#max,
conditional literals, and the five item shapes of 2.3. Polarity comes from
Literal.sign, never from prose — 2.2's recorded killer.

A MISSING GLOSS IS NOT A MISSING TEMPLATE. An earlier draft let an unglossed
atom fall back to layer 1, which reported 10 of 106 real items as unsupported
ASP when every one was an ordinary unary atom nobody had written a meaning for.
Unglossed names now render as a marked hole at layer 2 and raise
readback-ungloss, which blocks the clause from every seat — it fires on m0053's
interactable_entity today, as 2.3 predicted.

RB1-RB5 as specified. RB4 is reported and stamps non-evidential; it never fails
a clause, because a threshold on an echo score is the scoring instrument open
question 3 forbids.

Two places the plan could not be executed as written, both reported rather than
papered over: 2.3's act/1 template needs a gloss no schema field holds and no
module supplies, so the act term is rendered as itself, marked, and noted (RB1
exempts the marked span by name); and RB1 as literally specified fires on
ordinary English nouns inside a gloss, so each finding now says whether the
label sat in the gloss or in the renderer's own text — 18 and 16 respectively on
the corpus, and collapsing them would misdirect every repair.

19/19 hand-written mutants killed. Suite 467 pass, none lost.

commit 3759e55441cba7258402898d24ccf05f39c67581
ordinal 90
date 2026-08-07 23:22:50 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject stage 4: the renderer is total, and the restrictions it justified are withdrawn
RULING (Matt, 2026-08-07): no restrictions on the ASP for rendering. The point
of the implementation is to let the model represent section logic without new
code for every new concept.

schema.py
  * REMOVED `_check_body`'s anonymous-variable rejection. Its whole stated
    ground was "the tool that renders a rule back into English cannot process
    it". Disproved twice: [RAN] clingo derives p(a) from `p(X) :- q(X,_).
    q(a,b).`; and ASP2CNL renders `_` by skipping hidden terms
    (`assignment(_,blue)` -> "an assignment with color equal to blue",
    CEUR Vol-4117 short_rcra_3.pdf, read in full). A body binds its own
    variables, so there is no safety ground either.
  * KEPT `_check_term`'s rejection, REGROUNDED. The brief asked for both to go.
    [RAN] says otherwise: every slot `_check_term` guards renders into a rule
    HEAD, and `_` in a head is unsafe whatever the body binds --
    `q(a). p(_) :- q(X).` -> "unsafe variables in:" and clingo refuses the whole
    file. The message now states the solver's ground, not the renderer's.
  * `_strip_strings` deleted (dead with the guard it served).
  * Audited and KEPT on independent grounds, each named in the report:
    unsafe-variable, BEHAVIOUR_NS, RESERVED, _TERM narrowness, read_back
    `_BAD_IN_TEXT`, body newline, body trailing full stop.

test_schema.py / mutate_schema.py
  * `test_an_anonymous_variable_in_a_BODY` deleted with its guard; the ALSO
    mutation `_check_body / anonymous variable` deleted with it.
  * The term-slot test is kept, renamed to say HEAD, and its comment now
    carries the clingo run instead of "failure mode 7".
  * NEW positive control: `_` in a body validates, renders and CLINGO LOADS the
    .lp -- so restoring the deleted guard breaks something.
  * mutation: 44 guards, 44 killed, 0 survivors, 0 errors (was 45/45/0).
    Not a lowered floor: one guard withdrawn on a disproved ground, its safety
    half still pinned, and the newly permitted case pinned positively.

STEP_stage4.md -> revision 2, §2.3 only
  * Two layers. Layer 1 is a total `clingo.ast` fallback -- `str(node)` is
    defined on every node, so a rendering always exists; [RAN] 12/12 constructs
    (aggregates, conditional literals, comparisons, intervals, pooling, choice,
    weak constraints, nested functions, `_`) with no node-type introspection.
    Layer 2 is the old template table, now OPTIONAL: missing template -> layer
    1, never an error.
  * Every rendering stamps `layer: 1|2`; layer-1 fraction printed even at zero.
    Precedent: RB4's `non-evidential` stamp -- stamp, don't fail.
  * ASP2CNL recorded as the reference for template shapes, with its NAMING
    MODEL rejected by name: it verbalises predicate and attribute NAMES, and
    Invariant 1 requires the definition, not the label -- adopting it would
    manufacture failure mode #4 inside the renderer, where no seat can see it.
  * Its round-trip through CNL2ASP recorded as SPECULATIVE and not a
    dependency, and weaker than the brief assumed: the paper claims round-trip
    only for PENGASP, and only for rules PENGASP itself produced.
  * `readback-ungloss` survives = MISSING GLOSS, never a missing template; the
    two are now separated in prose and in test row 7b.
  * §8 gains rows 7b, 23-26.

commit b7c663e056a2d1f5b050d71d067773e6fea496a5
ordinal 98
date 2026-08-07 23:47:48 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject RESTORE the anonymous-variable guard: I withdrew it on the wrong tool
The spec-drift review caught this and it is my error. I briefed the withdrawal
as "the ground is disproved twice over", citing bare clingo (which does derive
from `p(X) :- q(X,_)`) and the published ASP2CNL renderer (which skips hidden
terms). Both claims are TRUE and both are about the wrong tool.

03_pipeline.md names XCLINGO, and STEP_stage4.md makes it load-bearing for
stage 4's R3 derivation layer. [RAN] xclingo 2.0b24:

  p(X) :- q(X,_).  ->  error: unsafe variables in:
                         _xclingo_sup(2,0,p(X),(#Anon0,X)):-...
  p(X) :- q(X,Y).  ->  |__"p holds of a"

xclingo lifts body variables into a supporting term where an anonymous one
becomes unsafe. It is not a limitation we can render around, and it takes the
WHOLE LINK SET's explanation down, not one rule. 03_pipeline.md:239 stage-2
node D1 still requires "no anonymous placeholders" -- the code now matches the
design again.

THE REPLACEMENT TEST RUNS XCLINGO rather than asserting the validator
complains. That is the whole difference: the old test checked only that
something raised, which is exactly what let the guard be withdrawn on a false
premise. The new one pins the GROUND, with a named-variable control proving the
failure is caused by the `_` and not by the fixture, and it refuses to pass
quietly if xclingo is missing.

test_readback keeps `_` in its direct-rendering list and excludes it from the
module-level one. The split is the point: the renderer must handle `_` (the
hand-written .lp files contain it) while the contract refuses to emit it.
Conflating "renderable" with "admissible" is what caused this.

Lesson, recorded because it cost a wrong commit: "the renderer can handle it"
is not a claim about renderers in general. Name the tool and run it.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

  CQ-6.c all reported `pass` with nothing executed: their expectations are

`p("political_content is bad")` was rewritten into prose — and a declared label
inside a string was glossed AWAY, blinding RB1, whose whole job is to see one.

is left alone (verified >= the true worst case for 1..5). The estimate's
surplus is exactly T(T-1)/2 copies of (system+user) — the same coefficient the
error-log term carries — so it covers the logs iff a log fits in one
system+user block. That is now a NAMED budget (8,000 chars; largest ever
produced: 1,294), priced into the hand-priced floor, and CHECKED.

Engineering findings of the stage-4 adversarial review (2026-08-08). Design

⛔ HIGH 3 — `mutate_seats.py` printed `83 mutants applied, 0 survivor(s)`,

reproduced before touching it. The kill rule was `returncode != 0` and nothing
else: a red suite kills every mutant, `rc=2` (a COLLECTION ERROR — the suite
did not run) read as KILLED, and nothing recorded which test died. The
documented pyc trap was genuinely fixed; the general shape was left standing
one line below the note about it (`DEBUGGING_TIPS` §8a).

Rewritten on `mutate_schema.py`'s design, which had all three guards in the
same directory the whole time: green baseline through the SAME isolation path,
return-code triage, collected-count comparison, and an `error` status — there
was none, so every failure folded into `killed`. `killed` now means A NAMED
TEST DIED, read off `-rfE`, which is what makes the entanglement table
possible. It no longer rewrites `seats.py` in place: each mutant goes into a
dot-prefixed mirror inside `paper_pipeline/`, and the source digest is
asserted. ⚠️ The mirror's LOCATION is load-bearing — a `/tmp` copy breaks
`WALKTHROUGH`/`link.py` and every mutant dies of an import error; recorded as
`DEBUGGING_TIPS` §15a because I took that path first.

`mutate_readback_r3.py` — R3 had NO committed harness, so `21813f2`'s "21/21
killed" was reproducible by nobody. Same engine, imported not copied.

  mutate_seats.py       100 mutants · 0 survivors · 0 errors

  mutate_schema.py       unchanged, still 0 survivors

Both harnesses' own guards are now pinned in `test_mutate.py` (red baseline

* the stage-3 cross-check had NO JOIN-KEY GUARD. 4d is keyed on claim
  SENTENCES, stage 3 on claim IDs; `[RAN]` on m0217 three numbers supplied,
  zero consulted, every `covered` stamped `unsupported` — byte-identical to
  "stage 3 never ran" — under `stage3_discrimination_available: True` with no
  warning on the line a human reads. A map that joins NOTHING is now refused;
  a PARTIAL miss is counted and printed (stage 3 may legitimately not reach a
  claim). The guard is in `build_report`, not `cross_check_4d`: that function
  is per-item and an absent key for one item is correctly not a zero.

  `unclear` rate pooled 4a in. §4.3(2) requires that line not to read 4a; the
  docstring was true of the KEY and false of the NUMBER. 4a's own rate stays
  in `by_seat`.

  `agreed_scope`, so an unexempted value scan refuses that clause's whole

commit 471c93bcfb42e220dfe0f05876418b266382186c
ordinal 115

author Matt Stults
subject stage 4 §6: delete the dead cross-seat divergence machinery; keep 4b-vs-4c
`divergences()` produced 0 records over all 81 legal verdict combinations, and
0 over all 255 combinations across every seat SUBSET. Verified independently
two ways: structurally (all six pairwise seat-vocabulary overlaps are DISJOINT
once `unclear` is removed, and both members of every `CONTRADICTIONS` pair sit
inside ONE seat) and behaviourally (exhaustive enumeration). The five tests
that pinned it built judgements `validate_judgements` refuses.

ROOT CAUSE — a transposition, not a coding error. `03_pipeline.md` §6's
divergence is between two reviewers answering the SAME question. This file
imported it as a mechanism across four seats asking DIFFERENT questions, which
do not share a unit: 4a/4b judge the whole rendering, 4c one licensed item, 4d
one claim. `4b: faithful` and `4c: unlicensed` can both be true.

REMOVED (all proved unreachable first): `CONTRADICTIONS`, `Divergence`,
`Triage`, `divergences`, `promote`, and `report_line`'s NOT-ADJUDICATED branch.
With them: 10 tests and 8 mutants, every one of which was pinning a guard
inside dead code. That is not floor-lowering — the mutants were mutating code
no legal input could reach, so the tests that killed them pinned nothing.

ADDED — the one check that earns its place. 4b is anchored to the RENDERING and
never sees the code; 4c is composed from the MODULE by `_item_text`, which
refuses any renderer mark, and 4c is never echo-stamped. So when they disagree
about ONE item, the only thing between them is the rendering ⇒ the INSTRUMENT
is accused, not the translation.

  * `POLARITY`, not verdict strings: faithful/licensed -> true,
    unfaithful/unlicensed -> false, `unclear` -> none. `unclear` from either
    side is NOT a disagreement — pricing honest hedging creates pressure toward
    false confidence, which is worse because it is invisible. A verdict in
    either closed set with no polarity is REFUSED, never read as an abstention.
  * `InstrumentDefect` carries both verdicts, both reasons, the rendering sha
    and both brief shas. The provenance is the point: the diagnosis accuses the
    apparatus, so the artifacts that produced the two answers must be re-read.
  * `INSTRUMENT_ORIGIN` is NOT in `DISCLOSABLE_ORIGINS`; the transcript fence
    refuses it and `route` tests it FIRST — otherwise the seat finding emitted
    by whichever seat said the negative thing would route `re-translate` and
    hand it back to the translator, asking a model to fix OUR renderer.
  * Neither verdict is rewritten. The deleted machinery replaced both with
    `unclear`, which erased 4c's verdict — the anchor — because a
    rendering-reading seat disagreed with it.

§4.3 IS UNAFFECTED and its two real mechanisms are unchanged and re-verified:
RB4's `ECHO_STAMPED = ("4b", "4d")` still excludes 4c, and `refuse_aggregate`
still refuses consensus at every nesting depth in keys and values, with 4a
still barred from the evidential seats. Removing divergence STRENGTHENS §4.1:
it was the only path by which a 4c verdict could be stamped and overwritten.

Does it fire today? No — no seat has ever been run, nothing has been spent.
Stated plainly, because a check that cannot fire is what is being replaced.
What is new is that it is REACHABLE: over the 7 stored modules that reach a
seat, 4b's denominator and 4c's judgeable set share every item id (5-18 per
clause), and both verdicts in a disagreement are legal for their own seat.

suite: 784 passed, 1 xfailed.  mutate_seats.py: 103 mutants, 0 survivors,
0 errors (100 -> 103; 8 removed with the dead code, 11 added).
test_seats.py: 134 -> 139 test functions (10 removed, 15 added).

author Matt Stults
subject artifact versioning: both hashes trigger a re-run, and a waiver that expires
`contract_hash` and `provenance_hash` had existed since the graveyard landed and
were called in exactly ONE place — metadata on a FAILURE record. Zero occurrences
across every runs/*/run.json and runs/*/m*.json. Nothing compared a stored hash to
a current one; nothing selected a clause for re-translation. The strategy was two
functions and a docstring.

Ruled by Matt 2026-08-08: BOTH hashes trigger a re-run, they stay separate, and an
intention flag may scope a partial re-run.

* every translated module is stamped: <clause>.version.json beside the object, the
  same two hashes per result in run.json, a `%` comment in the .lp. Not a field of
  the module: that object is a contract the MODEL satisfies, and provenance is a
  fact about the run.
* version.py classifies every stored module as current / provenance-stale /
  contract-stale / unstamped / no-longer-in-corpus. Both hashes moved => the state
  is contract-stale, because that is the one no waiver may excuse. Best state
  across runs wins, or nothing translated under an older prompt is ever current.
* translate.py --only-stale (off by default; changing what a bare run translates is
  a spend change) prints the census, by class with its denominator, ABOVE the cost
  line and narrows the estimate and the gate.
* --waivers PATH: a reviewed file with who/date/why naming the exact hash
  transition, so it expires the next time the prompt moves. Enumerated ids only,
  never a contract change (it refuses the run), never an unstamped artifact, inert
  without --only-stale, and an unused waiver is reported loudly.
* mutate_version.py: 34 mutants over version.py, graveyard.py and the wiring in
  translate.py, 0 survivors. Its first run found 8 survivors — four of them behind
  tests that read as thorough and were satisfied by pytest's tmp_path echoing the
  test's own name into the captured output.

Census over the stored runs today: 19 stored modules, 11 distinct clauses, all
`unstamped` — no module predating this commit records what it was made from.

commit 9045a8a47e588943668772aca1a89dedac3f284a
ordinal 119
date 2026-08-08 07:28:18 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Replace two guards with a transform and a defect check -- and my premise was wrong
Matt's ruling: a restriction justified by a TOOL should first be tested for a
meaning-preserving transform, and a guard should test the DEFECT rather than a
character that correlates with it. Both applied.

THE `_` GUARD IS NOW A TRANSFORM. deanonymise() substitutes a distinct fresh
variable per `_` in the text handed to xclingo. Not persisted: render_lp's .lp,
the stored module and ModuleR3.program all keep the author's `_`. The property
that licenses the whole change is pinned directly -- both programs run through
clingo and their witness sets compared, over 9 rows, asserting SATISFIABLE
BEFORE comparing, because two programs that both fail to ground compare equal
and would prove nothing.

MY PREMISE WAS FALSE AND THE AGENT CAUGHT IT. I briefed "an anonymous variable
IS a fresh variable used once, so renaming is alpha-renaming, provably". That
holds for POSITIVE literals only. [RAN]

  p(X) :- q(X,_),   not r(X,_).     -> p(b)
  p(X) :- q(X,_V1), not r(X,_V2).   -> error: '_V2' is unsafe

gringo PROJECTS a negative literal rather than renaming it, so the transform
would turn a solvable program into a refused one. deanonymise skips any literal
containing `not`. And xclingo renders those unaided, so that case never needed
a guard or a transform -- the restriction was wider than its own ground all
along.

THE FULL-STOP GUARD NOW COUNTS STATEMENTS. It parses the body and counts Rule
nodes: severed -> 2, comma -> 1, 1..3 -> 1, #count{} -> 1, "a.b" -> 1. No
special-casing, no false positives, and _strip_strings is deleted -- it existed
only to prop up the two character-matching guards. The old test asserted the
message said "full stop", which passes against either implementation; it now
asserts "produced 2 statements".

Plus bad worked example 6 teaching the severed form, and a 19-line standing
comment above _check_term so the next model does not reach for a restriction
before testing for a transform.

THE SWEEP FOUND MORE. _check_body's newline ban is the same class and has the
same remedy (report only, not acted on). And the brace half of _BAD_IN_TEXT is
grounded on something FALSE: xclingo accepts { and } raw inside a trace_rule
literal, and escaping them breaks it. Quotes and backslashes escape correctly
but xclingo's tree printer emits the literal source bytes, so a seat would read
\"exception\" -- escaping is only half a transform without an unescape on the
parse side, which does not exist.

The agent's own mutation sweep caught it writing a vacuous test inside the
change that cites the rule against vacuous tests.

784 passed. mutate_schema 46 guards 0 survivors; mutate_readback_r3 41 mutants
0 survivors. One mutant removed with its guard -- the obligation moved rather
than lapsed, and mutate_readback_r3 gained 8.

schema.py and prompt/20_worked_example.md both need Matt's guard.py --accept.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

subject design: the artifact-versioning ruling, and section 6 is about ONE question
Two edits, +113 lines, reviewed and accepted by Matt.

ARTIFACT VERSIONING. Both hashes trigger a re-run; contract_hash is not
optional. The intention flag is a reviewed, diffable waiver file naming both
ends of the provenance transition, so it expires by construction and cannot
excuse a contract change or an unstamped artifact. Rejected by name: "one hash
for everything", "a provenance change only relabels, never re-runs", "an
unstamped artifact is current until shown otherwise", "put the version inside
the module object", and "a --force-current switch".

SECTION 6 CLARIFIED. It is about REPLICATE JUDGEMENTS ON ONE QUESTION -- two
reviewers, one brief, one item -- not a mechanism for comparing seats that ask
different questions. Two seats answering different questions differently is not
a contradiction: 4b faithful and 4c unlicensed can both be true, since a
rendering can faithfully render an item that lacks a valid citation.

The one cross-seat comparison that IS meaningful is recorded with its ground:
4b is anchored to the rendering and never sees the code, 4c is composed from the
module and refuses any renderer mark, so a disagreement accuses the RENDERING
and routes to a human rather than back to the translator.

And the misreading is recorded in one sentence because it will recur: a
within-question mechanism was applied across questions.

Matt asked whether we can keep the context needed to tell apart two concepts
established in one passage. Checking the stored modules to answer it overturned
a claim this log and OPEN_QUESTIONS.md Q-6 were both repeating.

interactable_entity and interaction_entity are NOT two names in one module. They
are one clause translated twice:

  run 20260807-143853:  defines(m0053, assistant, interaction_entity).
  run 20260807-154618:  defines(m0053, assistant, interactable_entity).

Same clause, same slot, same kind, assistant glossed identically in both. Only
the coined term differs. Not a concept-identity failure -- run-to-run instability
in one field.

And it is general. Across every clause translated more than once:
  concept names (what a clause introduces): 0.30 agreement (11 of 37)
  borrowed names (what a clause NEEDS):     0.00 agreement (0 of 22)

Zero of twenty-two borrowed names are coined the same way twice. The twins are
not a special case -- every borrowed name is a twin. Glosses meanwhile are
stable: of 45 seen in more than one run only 6 differ, and all 6 are paraphrase.
The model agrees about the meaning and disagrees about the label, every time.

So the tension Matt asked about is not real, because it is two jobs needing
different information from different sources. Differentiating two conditions in
one sentence needs the DOCUMENT, and clause-blind grounding already does it (P4
separated at 0.00 in 2 of 3 runs, 97% pairwise stability). Knowing two names are
one concept needs OUR RUN STORE, mechanically: same clause + same slot implies
same concept by construction, no model and no retrieval.

The confound never came from having clause context. It came from asking one
model in one pass to do both jobs while holding the clause, at which point
provenance answers both cheaply, and it did.

Records three designs that keep context without the confound -- sibling-blind
clause-visible, ground-blind-then-discriminate, and never-ask-the-model-to-merge
-- with the last as the one the measurements support. Nothing decided.

commit af5aac44b96b8eea9895adc6acf61f15811d4438
ordinal 133
date 2026-08-09 07:37:06 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject design: (b) ground blind, then discriminate with context -- written, not built
Matt asked for design (b) laid out clearly. Written to the working method: a
specific passing example, a specific failing example, what evidence it produces,
and what it costs.

The idea: stage 1 fixes spans with no clause in the room (already run, 97%
pairwise stability). Stage 2 sees the clause but may only NARROW what stage 1
chose. Context can then discriminate and cannot manufacture.

The narrow-only contract is the whole design. Per name stage 2 emits keep,
narrow, or flag; a narrowed span must be a contiguous SUBSTRING of the stage-1
span, checked mechanically. It may not widen, replace, add, or ground a name
stage 1 left ungrounded -- all of those need retrieval, and retrieval with the
clause visible is the confound. Without the substring check this is the
confounded design with extra steps.

Passing example is m0150 from blind_run2, where stage 1 put three different
conditions from one sentence at 1.00 and 0.50 similarity. Correct narrowing
makes all three disjoint. The key is not mine: blind runs 1 and 3 independently
produced the separated form, having never seen the clause.

Failing example is stage 2 replacing a span with a better-reading one from the
clause's home section -- the confound returning -- caught mechanically because
it is not a substring. Also states the failure the check does NOT catch:
sub-spans that are disjoint and wrong, which only the cross-run key and reading
the list will find. Hence the work list must stay readable: measured at 7-9 pairs
across 7-8 clauses per run.

The deciding measure is directional. Clause context RAISED same-clause
similarity to 100% under the old design; under (b) it must FALL from the blind
baseline of 86/75/50%. If it rises, the design failed in the way it exists to
prevent and no other number rescues it.

Five pre-registered predictions, D1 and D3 named as falsifiers. Cost: free.
