commit 27ca87ff7f41700c5b8d62ba1f26cd0cabac52f7
ordinal 134
date 2026-08-09 07:43:51 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Q-22 DEFECT: a borrowed predicate can vanish from the situation signature
Found while answering Matt's "are we off track?". It is the answer.

probe.situation_signature is inputs UNION (head-less predicates DECLARED IN THE
CONCEPT TABLE). A borrowed predicate is head-less and, per Q-6, is exactly what
the concept table does not cover. It matches neither term and is silently
dropped: no module derives it, no situation sets it, nothing reports it missing.

On committed m0079, which references conflicts_with_higher_authority/1 while the
concept table declares the differently-named higher_authority_conflict/1:

  conflicts_with_higher_authority(I) can never be true
  => higher_authority_conflict(I) can never be true
  => not higher_authority_conflict(I) is ALWAYS true
  => the module collapses to "every instruction is applicable"

The clause's entire content -- instructions being superseded by higher authority
-- is inert, and the probe reads GREEN because the predicate is not in the
signature, so no enumerated situation can test the dead branch.

Scale: 19 modules examined, 6 (32%) have a referenced head-less predicate
missing from the signature. In 1 the orphan gates a negated literal (always-true
branch, wrong output); in 5 it is positive-only (rule never fires, missing
output). Both silent. m0255 loses out_of_scope/2, policy_class/2, scope/2;
m0105 loses four; m0134 loses task/1; m0150 loses translation_of_user_content/1.

This reframes the resolve_runs line. Satisfying a required input has three
discharge routes and every experiment pursued only the second: (1) declare it as
a situation input, which needs no document and no model and is where 32% of
modules fail; (2) another module derives it, needing cross-module identity --
names cannot do it at 0.00, extension is the only stable key at 97%; (3)
genuinely undischargeable, which must become an explicit open input rather than
silence. A predicate does not need a meaning to be satisfied. It needs to be
declared. The resolution work was not wrong, it was premature.

Two candidate fixes recorded, neither chosen: widen the signature rule (watch
max_signature=10), or widen the stage-1 contract (Q-6, manufactures problem #9).
Independent of which: an orphan reaching neither route must be a hard error, not
a silent drop.

author Matt Stults
subject resolve: assessment of Matt's two routes -- the blocker is corpus size, not method
Matt: linkage may have no generic solution; either give the model all the terms
and ask it to normalize naming, or write falsification tests for name

The suspicion is supported, but the sharper statement is stronger: linkage is
not a property of the document, it is a property of our translation. Two modules
link because they use the same symbol, and that is under our control. Every
experiment in the log was trying to read a join key out of prose that never
contained one, which is why they returned 0.06 then 0.00.

BUT the measurement that would test it is currently unrunnable. 0 of 12 orphan
predicates have an exact head match anywhere -- and only 13 of 593 clauses
(2.2%) are translated. The clauses that would DEFINE those symbols mostly do not
exist yet. 0/12 is a corpus-size artifact, not a finding about naming.

Route A is the right shape -- a closed-world partition over a finite given set
rather than open retrieval -- but blocked twice: all 12 orphans carry gloss "-",
because the concept table covers only names a clause introduces (Q-6), so a
normalizer would match bare identifier strings, which are the 0.00-stable thing;
and cross-module matches cannot exist at 2.2% coverage.

Corrects my own earlier framing before it gets built on: normalization would NOT
fix m0079's pair and merging would be wrong. higher_authority_conflict is a
derived concept defined FROM the primitive conflicts_with_higher_authority;
merging head and body gives p :- p. Most orphans are route 1 (declare), not
route 2 (link), so normalization's prize is smaller than the orphan count
suggests.

Route B is the stronger route and better than it looks, because the test already
exists and is objective: the orphan count. That is the first closed loop with a
mechanical score in this line of work. Records five available falsifiers, all
free, and the risk -- iterating until tests pass is fitting, and with 13 clauses
there is no held-out set to draw (DEBUGGING_TIPS 3).

commit 0f614f16818995767985674c95b6617404b9844c
ordinal 136
date 2026-08-09 08:14:04 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Q-22 CORRECTED + Q-23: why can't a borrow carry a gloss?
Q-22 correction, same day, after reading the modules instead of the signature
rule. The first version said the stage-1 contract had a hole and borrowed
predicates were undeclared. Wrong, and it moves the fix.

Every orphan IS declared, in `requires`. schema.py's D4b check -- "body
references X but nothing declares it" -- passed correctly; the model separated
requires ("another clause must define it") from inputs ("supplied with the
case") sensibly. The defect is that probe.situation_signature reads inputs and
ignores requires entirely, so a requires predicate is correctly declared, not in
the signature, and at 13 of 593 clauses not defined by any link either --
silently false in every enumerated situation. schema.py deferred this to link
time in a comment, and link time never implemented it. An unsatisfiable promise
is indistinguishable from a kept one.

Q-23 answers Matt's question. The recorded objection is that a borrower glossing
what it borrows invents a meaning for a term another clause owns, manufacturing
problem #9. That assumes gloss divergence, and the measurement says the
opposite: borrowed names agree at 0.00 across repeat translations while 39 of 45
concept glosses are identical and all 6 differences are paraphrase. The model
agrees about meaning and disagrees about labels, every time.

And a borrow gloss is a different speech act. concepts[].gloss says "X means
this" and is authoritative; a gloss on requires says "I need an X that means
this" and is an expectation owned by nobody. Two clauses may hold different
expectations without contradiction, and divergence between them DETECTS problem
#9 rather than creating it. It is manufactured only if the borrow gloss is typed
as a definition.

Independent argument: requires is currently an unverifiable promise. Nothing
checks it, and even once a clause defines something, nothing can tell whether it
is the same thing, because names agree at 0.00. A gloss is the only artifact
that could make the link checkable.

Cost: schema.py is watched, so guard acceptance plus a contract_hash re-translate
-- which at 13 of 593 clauses is near its lifetime minimum.

commit b18d3936e3f4ff49cfe30801fff448a231db1cdb
ordinal 137
date 2026-08-09 08:32:22 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject experiment: the linkage experiment, staged behind a cheap falsifier -- proposal only
Matt asked for the full experiment and how it fits the goal.

The goal needs chains: a behaviour touches clause A, A borrows disallowed/1, B
defines it, so B bears on the behaviour too. Without that the tool returns
isolated clauses and silently misses everything one hop away. So the
goal-critical question is not what a borrowed symbol MEANS but whether it can be
DISCHARGED. Every experiment in the log answered the first question and returned
0.06 then 0.00. Today 0 of 12 orphans find a provider anywhere -- the chain does
not form even once.

That cannot be measured yet: 13 of 593 clauses are translated, so 0/12 is a
corpus-size artifact. And random sampling would be the wrong draw -- linking is a
neighbourhood phenomenon, so a random scatter across 78 sections yields a graph
with no edges by construction and would falsely confirm failure. Translate whole
sections.

Two changes under test: a gloss on requires, typed explicitly as an EXPECTATION
rather than a definition (mistyping it is what makes Q-6's problem-#9 objection
correct); and an unsatisfied requires becoming a reported state rather than a
validation error, since making it fatal would fail every module at 2.2% coverage.

Staged so the cheap falsifier runs first. Stage 0 re-translates only the existing
13 clauses, twice, and asks whether two translations of one clause write
COMPATIBLE expectations for the same borrowed symbol. Concept glosses are 39/45
identical across repeats; borrow glosses have never existed and are unmeasured.
If they behave like borrow NAMES (0.00), stop -- no corpus buys a link. Cost
~$0.07 at the measured $0.005/module, versus ~$3.00 for the same contract change
after full translation.

Stage 1 translates 162 clauses across ten sections chosen because the orphans and
the 43 borrowed predicates point into them, ~$0.81, with ignore_untrusted_data
and protect_privileged_information held out because no orphan names them.

Six pre-registered predictions; L0 is the stage-0 falsifier and L5 requires the
held-out sections. Records what it does not do: symbols the document never
defines stay open inputs, and success makes that set smaller and visible, not
empty.

Total ~$0.88, of which ~$0.07 is spent before the falsifier can stop it.

commit 8758d2dd409c3d22b24e86327d4feeb4cc94cca5
ordinal 138
date 2026-08-09 08:42:08 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject experiment: REVISION -- the discharge route is a coin flip, so collect the route guess too
Matt asked to validate the experiment before paying, and to consider what other
experiments need so data is collected once. The validation ran free on disk.

It says the design was wrong. Two of two repeat-translated clauses with borrows
show a WHOLESALE flip of requires vs inputs -- one run puts everything in inputs,
the other everything in requires. Since inputs reaches the signature and requires
does not (Q-22), the same clause is testable or inert depending on which field
the model picked, and the test space differs by 8x (m0150: 16 situations vs 2).
So glossing `requires` would polish a field whose membership is arbitrary.

This is not a model failure. requires means "another clause defines it", which is
a GLOBAL property of the corpus, not a local property of one clause. A translator
seeing one clause cannot know whether any of the other 592 defines pasted_text/1.
We were asking it to guess, forcing the choice, and routing the whole test space
on the guess. Route assignment belongs at link time, where the corpus is visible.

It also explains the rest of the log with no new hypotheses: names are noise
(0.00) because nothing constrains them; glosses are stable (39/45) because
meaning is local and visible; providers are 0/12 because at 2.2% there are none.

Revised collection follows Matt's economics -- the cost driver is the translation
PASS, not the field count, so take the union of what candidate experiments need
and collect once. One `borrows` list replaces requires/inputs, carrying name,
arity, an expectation gloss, an expected_discharge HINT recorded but not acted
on, and an optional expected_section.

The two load-bearing design points: recording the route guess without acting on
it means the signature is computed from ALL borrows, so no module can be silently
inert (Q-22 dissolves) while E3 measures whether the guess was worth trusting;
and allowing `unsure` converts a forced coin flip into recorded uncertainty.

States the honest cost of collecting wide: a larger output schema is a harder
generation task. Adds L7 as a second falsifier -- if abstention and repair rates
rise materially, the union is the wrong batch and must be cut to the gloss alone.

commit adc49e57aede6501e578d08bae09e91dd2069e91
ordinal 139
date 2026-08-09 08:43:53 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject DEBUGGING_TIPS 19: the worked example teaches the opposite of the prose on requires/inputs
Matt, on the measured wholesale flip: "that seems like we're not defining inputs
and requires well enough in the prompt." Correct, and the section-1 diagnostic
finds it in one grep.

The prose criterion is clear and locally decidable -- requires is what another
clause must define, inputs describes the case being judged. The worked example
contradicts it: 20_worked_example.md's m0088 block emits "requires": [] and puts
all six body predicates in inputs, then says approvingly "All six appear in
inputs". Among them same_level/2 (authority levels are established by
levels_of_authority) and misaligned_with_higher_level/1 (needs the chain of
command) are document-side by the prompt's own rule.

And those are exactly the concepts m0079 later coined as
conflicts_with_higher_authority/1 and conflicts_with_later_same_authority/1 and
placed in requires. Two translations, two fields, both following the prompt,
because the prompt demonstrates one answer and states the other.

Corrects the diagnosis I filed earlier today. I recorded this as not a model
failure -- requires being a global property of the corpus the translator cannot
know -- and proposed a schema change to collapse the two fields. Half true, and
it pointed at the expensive fix. Whether another clause actually defines the
symbol is global, but that is not the prompt's criterion; the prompt asks
"document or case?", which is local and decidable from the clause alone. The
model was following a contradictory example, exactly as section 1 predicts.

The generalisable lesson: check the DEMONSTRATION before concluding the task is
impossible. A contradictory example and an unknowable distinction both produce a
stable-looking coin flip from the outside, and they have wildly different fixes.

The fix is a prompt change (provenance_hash, modules stay valid) not a contract
change (contract_hash, modules may no longer validate). Same ~$0.07
re-translation, no structural risk. If fixing the example kills the flip, the
borrows collapse may be unnecessary entirely -- test the cheap fix first.

Notes that test_prompt_examples.py counts demonstrated shapes mechanically but
does not count requires/inputs placement, which is why this survived.

commit 818045f4c36c059594a0fa58a79d292ee368d400
ordinal 140
date 2026-08-09 11:37:03 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Q-22 FIX: an unsatisfied `requires` now reaches the situation signature
situation_signature was inputs UNION (head-less AND declared-in-concepts). A
borrowed predicate is head-less and the concept table covers only names a clause
INTRODUCES, so `requires` matched neither term and was silently dropped. 6 of 19
stored modules carried one.

On m0079 the dropped predicate sat under a negation, so
not higher_authority_conflict(I) was permanently true and the module collapsed
to "every instruction is applicable" -- the clause's entire content inert, probe
green, because no enumerated situation could reach the dead branch.

Measured before/after on the stored corpus, 40 -> 55 signature predicates:
  m0255-1339  5 -> 8    m0079-1719  1 -> 3    m0079-1748  1 -> 5
  m0105-1748  1 -> 5    m0134-1748  5 -> 6    m0150-1748  1 -> 2

m0079-1719 now enumerates 8 situations instead of 2, derives
higher_authority_conflict(x) and later_same_authority_conflict(x) in some of
them, and applicable_instruction discriminates instead of being permanently
true.

The fix is NOT "add requires to the signature". requires means another clause
defines it, and when a linked module does define it the predicate is DERIVED;
putting a derived predicate in the signature lets a situation assert it
independently of the rule that produces it, which is worse than the bug being
fixed. So an unsatisfied requires joins and a satisfied one does not, pinned by
test_q22_control_a_requires_THE_LINK_DEFINES_stays_out.

A fourth term was needed too: linking a provider in moved the required predicate
out of the signature correctly while the provider's OWN inputs never entered, so
the defining rule could not fire either and linking made a module MORE inert.
Linked modules' inputs are facts about the same case and now join.

Reported, not silently repaired: a `requires-unsatisfied` note names each unkept
promise. severity=note deliberately, not error -- only error drives the stage-2
repair loop and no rewrite of this module can conjure the clause that was
supposed to define the predicate (DEBUGGING_TIPS 7).

Consequence, surfaced not suppressed: with borrows now enumerated,
render_situation refuses them for want of a gloss (Q-6), which blocked two
shipped specs. The gloss fence is the anti-hollow-stub check and was NOT
relaxed. probe_live gains UnglossedSignature and blocking_gaps so a blocked
module is a named, reported state; two new tests prove every block is genuine
(the predicate really is in requires and really is unglossed) and that the
error names the predicate. No count is pinned -- Q-23 landing will legitimately
empty that set.

730 pass, 1 xfailed.

commit ed2bbafda7897f43863944c1ad08d1d522e56be6
ordinal 141
date 2026-08-09 13:34:16 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject Q-22 FIX: a borrowed condition can no longer vanish from the test space
The machinery half. The worked-example fix and its three placement tests are
held back together, since those tests fail against the old prompt and would make
a clean checkout red.

THE BUG. A logic module often needs a condition it does not define, and declares
it in `requires` -- "another clause defines this". situation_signature ignored
that field entirely, so the condition was silently treated as never true and any
rule depending on it could never fire. 6 of 19 stored modules carried one.

On m0079 the dropped condition sat under a negation, so
`not higher_authority_conflict(I)` was permanently true and the module collapsed
from "follow the instruction unless a higher authority conflicts" to "always
follow the instruction". The probe reported PASSED, because with the condition
absent from the signature no enumerated situation could reach the dead branch.

THE FIX is not "add requires to the signature". When a linked module DOES define
the predicate it is derived, and putting a derived predicate in the signature
lets a situation assert it independently of the rule that produces it -- worse
than the bug. So an unsatisfied requires joins and a satisfied one does not,
pinned by test_q22_control_a_requires_THE_LINK_DEFINES_stays_out.

A fourth term was needed too: linking a provider in moved the required predicate
out of the signature correctly, while the provider's OWN inputs never entered,
so the defining rule could not fire either and linking made a module MORE inert.

Measured on the stored corpus, 40 -> 55 signature predicates. m0079 went from 2
enumerated situations to 8, now derives both conflict predicates, and
applicable_instruction discriminates instead of being constant.

Reported, not silently repaired: a `requires-unsatisfied` note names each unkept
promise. severity=note not error -- only error drives the stage-2 repair loop,
and no rewrite of this module can conjure the clause that was supposed to define
the predicate (DEBUGGING_TIPS 7).

CONSEQUENCE, surfaced not suppressed. With borrows enumerated, render_situation
refuses them for want of a gloss, blocking two shipped specs. The gloss fence is
the anti-hollow-stub check and was NOT relaxed. probe_live gains
UnglossedSignature and blocking_gaps so a blocked module is a named, reported
state; two tests prove every block is genuine and that the error names the
predicate. No count is pinned. This makes a borrow gloss a PREREQUISITE for
stage 4, not an optimisation.

Some modules now FAIL that previously passed -- they passed by having nothing to
test. A pass count alone will misread this as a regression.

Includes four live translation runs ($0.0318) and the measurement write-up.

730 pass, 1 xfailed.

subject preflight: free Haiku run of the borrow-description change -- two findings, no spend
Matt asked for a manual run against Haiku before real spend. Built the real
system and user prompts via translate.build_system/build_user, handed them

two runs each, $0.00.

worked example and never wrote the instruction down. Every clause with a
borrowed condition would have been rejected for a rule it was never told,

instruction was clear.

m0079 run A produced three binary relations (higher_authority/2, conflicts/2,
same_authority/2); run B produced two unary bundles
(conflicts_with_higher_authority/1, superseded_by_later_same_level/1). B's
single condition is approximately A's two conjoined, and the ARITIES differ, so
they cannot pair one-to-one even in principle. Borrowed names shared between
runs: 0 of 8 on m0079, 0 of 7 on m0150. This is a layer of disagreement
underneath naming that no gloss quality removes.

CORRECTION: the paid DeepSeek runs measured borrowed-name agreement rising
0.00 -> 0.50/0.69 after the worked-example fix, reported with the caveat that it
was unpredicted and should be re-measured. Re-measured here: 0.00 and 0.00.
Either Haiku varies more or the earlier figure was noise at 9-16 symbols. It
must not be relied on.

Keep the description change -- cheap, followable, readable, and it unblocks the
two specs Q-22 left blocked; its value does not depend on linking. Do NOT yet
spend on translating whole sections for linking: the plan assumed one condition
matches one definition, and that is not the shape of the problem.

Records three untested options, favouring matching at the CLAUSE level rather
than the predicate level -- the only one that does not require the linking
problem solved first, and closer to what the tool actually claims.

commit c3f6922034cc83c7a4294a52d78b003055d1b1e4
ordinal 220
date 2026-08-15 12:08:30 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject gen-12 round FROZEN: restart-on-repeat chain policy + arity check, reviewed and offline-verified
Commits the exact state the next slice will run with, so the run is
reproducible against one contract.

CODE (uncommitted until now; suite green at 877 passed / 1 pre-existing
xfail, plus 411 in resolve_runs):

* Restart-on-repeat chain policy. A repair chain that emits a reply
  byte-identical to an earlier one in the same chain is frozen: the chain is
  discarded and redrawn once from attempt 1. Capped at ONE restart per
  clause -- a refreeze abandons rather than restarting again, so the worst
  case is a genuine bound of two chains, not a floor.
* Arity-aware declaration check in checks.py.

FOUR FINDINGS FROM A CLEAN-CONTEXT ADVERSARIAL REVIEW, each reproduced
independently before it was applied (this repo's rule: adversarial reviews
find false positives):

* D1/F1 cost gate. estimate_cost carries TWO quadratic terms, so the
  rejected `estimate_cost(..., 2T)` shim over-charged ~4.5x and refused
  every live config. Correct pricing is estimate_cost(T) DOUBLED. The P5 pin
  is now parametrised by glob over all 8 reachable config*.json, asserting
  the relation only -- never a pinned dollar figure. It caught that
  config_graph_nodes.json ($1.9940 vs its $1.00 ceiling) was refused by its
  own gate.
* F2 prev_shape hole. Flags were cleared at the restart but prev_shape was
  not, so a non-parsing redraw retained the DISCARDED draft's shape and the
  next module was diffed against bytes nobody kept -- force-keeping a clause
  into the graveyard on a discarded draft's flag. Fixed; pinned on the
  non-parsing path specifically.
* F3 docstring stated the rejected 2T shim as current behaviour.
* F4 the seam guard can vary the redraw's body; ACCEPTED and recorded rather
  than repaired, with the reachable path written down.

CEILING, raised by the human, minimal by ruling: config_graph_nodes.json
1.00 -> 2.00 exactly (not 2.50, not "with headroom" -- a ceiling with slack
has stopped being a constraint). config_corpus_all.json untouched at $8.00;
the ruling is more, smaller slices (~125 nodes, halved by the doubling).

OFFLINE VERIFICATION (preflight_replay/, zero spend):
* Arity check: 0 false positives on 237 accepted module files, 230 accepted
  final attempts, 120 first-try successes. No accepted module is pulled into
  a repair loop.
* Restart blast radius: 52 of 212 multi-attempt chains fire. Cost multiplier
  measured at 0.89x-1.28x, NOT 2x -- the truncated chain nearly pays for the
  redraw. The est*2 in run() is a gate bound, not a spend forecast.
* 4 chains that SUCCEEDED would have been restarted (7.7% of fired chains),
  inside the 9-12% band the redraw-not-abandon ruling was made against.
* Resumption measured, not inferred: the stamp is written on the success
  path only, so a discarded chain can never make a resume skip real work.

LIVE VALIDATION (20260815-113545, $0.0119 / 7 calls, criteria pre-registered
per clause BEFORE the run): 6 translated, 0 failed, flags empty, no restart.
NO REGRESSION -- and explicitly NOT a validation of either new path: no
chain froze and no module carried an arity mismatch, so neither mechanism
was entered. Rejected by name: reading 6/6 translated as validation of the
chain policy. The cohort went quiet because it was selected on OLD-PROMPT
failures and the committed prompt changes appear to have fixed them
(l171_426_n024: unrepaired-after-5-attempts -> translated on attempt 1).

ANALYSIS COMMITTED, NOT ACTED ON: the abstention-boundary measurement and
its cross-model check. The 32% over-translation FIGURE is WITHDRAWN (kappa
0.248 between judges); the DIRECTION survives and the circularity worry is
refuted by direction (15/15 discordant pairs run toward the stricter
verdict, McNemar p = 6.1e-5). What needs no judge at all is documentary: the
prompt contradicts itself on abstain-vs-translate, and DeepSeek gave both
answers to a clause whose translated exemplar sits in its own system prompt.
No prompt file is touched by this commit.

subject guard: fence the graph-node worked example -- it was substituted for a watched file and inherited none of the watching
Found by asking why a gen-12 commit touched no watched file when the
assembled system prompt had demonstrably moved (28,091 -> 37,891 chars).

THE GAP. config_corpus_all.json builds its system prompt from four files:
00_task.md, 10_output_format.md, **node_worked_example.md**, and
30_failure_modes.md -- and lists the watched prompt/20_worked_example.md as
UNUSED. The graph-node run swaps its own worked example in for the watched
one. Three of the four files instructing the corpus run were fenced; the
fourth was not, purely because it lives beside the graph configs instead of
in prompt/.

WHY IT MATTERS MORE THAN THE OTHER THREE: a worked example is a
DEMONSTRATION, and a demonstration overrides prose because it is what the
model imitates. That is not speculation here -- this file currently
contradicts 00_task.md's abstention criterion (00_task.md:110 makes a
section heading grounds to abstain; node_worked_example.md shows a heading
node translated and says a hollow module or a clean abstention "are both
better than an invented obligation"), and a production model gave BOTH
answers to a clause whose translated exemplar sits in its own system prompt.

Nothing has slipped through: both files last moved together in 3d856a8. The
gap is live, not historical -- the drafted abstention fix edits this very
file, and under the old list it would have landed with no human review.

Registered as a glob (node_*.md) so a future node-side prompt file is fenced
on arrival rather than after the fact. Per this repo's rule that
registration, not documentation, fences a thing.

DELIBERATELY NOT ACCEPTED. The guard now reports the file as NEVER REVIEWED,
which is the correct state: `--accept` is a human act recorded with a name,
and attesting a file whose self-contradiction we just documented would be
the exact failure the guard exists to prevent, performed by hand.

commit 10911de7fbf256ea6309ec137610038d32fd359f
ordinal 224
date 2026-08-15 15:58:35 -0700
author Matt Stults
subject prefer-polarity detector: catch the class stage 4 structurally cannot
A stage-4 read of 25 modules found the corpus asserting the OPPOSITE of the
specification, in modules that passed schema, clingo, link checks and
read-back. Verbatim, l1974_2125_n019.lp:57, from a BAD-marked example of
escalating emotional closeness with a lonely user:

    asserts(l1974_2125_n019, prefer, respond_with(R))
        :- escalates_emotional_closeness(R).

ROOT CAUSE IS A CONTRACT GAP, NOT A MODEL SLIP: `status` has no negative pole.
With a single-act `asserts` there is NO CORRECT ENCODING for "avoid X", and the
model reliably picks the one that inverts.

WHY NOTHING ELSE CATCHES IT, INCLUDING ALL FOUR STAGE-4 SEATS: the read-back
prose is CORRECT ("responding with % is dispreferred"); only `status` is wrong.
Seats 4a-4d all judge the English RENDERING -- 4b is explicitly not shown the
program at all -- so a defect living in the gap between a correct rendering and
the field it was rendered from is invisible to every one of them. That is why
this check is mechanical: it compares an entry's `status` against its OWN
read-back, a self-contradiction inside one object.

MEASURED over every stored module: 9 entries across 7 clauses.

⛔ ORIGIN IS `stage4-detector`, DELIBERATELY *NOT* DISCLOSABLE. Every instinct
says a self-contradiction inside one entry is stage-2-shaped and belongs in the
repair loop. It must not go there YET, because with no negative pole a model
told "these two disagree" cannot resolve it correctly: it will either drop the
entry (deleting the document's guidance) or rewrite the read-back to say
"preferred" -- silencing the only surviving evidence and leaving a module that
is semantically backwards AND internally consistent. That is strictly worse
than today and is textbook defect-trading. The precondition for promoting it to
a disclosable stage-2 check is a negative pole in `status`, which is an owner
ruling on guard-watched schema.py; the origin changes to "schema" in the same
commit that adds the pole.

Suite: 877 passed, 1 pre-existing xfail.

D3 -- node_worked_example.md, 9 lines, all JSON, all inside the demonstrations.

  Finding 1 (VERIFIED). `l4251_4571_n029` shipped `"requires": []` while its
  node contract carries `NEEDS: voice_turn_taking_rule` -- the file's own
  contract #2 violated in the demonstration the model imitates, and failure
  mode #2 (missing cross-reference) taught as acceptable. Fixed: the NEEDS
  name is in `requires` with its `concepts` gloss.

  Finding 3b (VERIFIED). The heading exemplar guarded its only rule on
  `rule_under_heading/2` in `requires`. That node's contract says
  `NEEDS: (none)`, so no node owns the name -- and measurably none does (0
  providers among the 773 live nodes) while 126 nodes NEED the
  `guideline_authority` this module was meant to supply. The head could never

  `inputs`, where a fact the module identifies about the material belongs.
  ⛔ The discriminator is the node's NEEDS block, NOT "no provider turned up" --
  the latter is checks.py's convergent-repair attack, and this is not it.

  Findings 2 and 4 are VERIFIED and NOT fixed. They are prose-only, and the
  file has no room: config_graph_nodes.json prices at $1.9940 against its own
  $2.00 ceiling, i.e. 383 bytes of headroom, and the two JSON fixes above spend
  277 of them ($1.9984 after). The full prose repair measured +3323 bytes and
  put the shipped config at $2.0460 -- `cost_gate` would REFUSE the run. Fixing
  them needs an owner ruling: raise the ceiling, or cut existing teaching prose
  to fund it. No floor was lowered here.

  Pins added, each mutation-tested against the defect it is for:
  requires == NEEDS for every translated exemplar (both directions -- missing
  names are failure mode #2, extra ones failure mode #3); every PROVIDES name
  reaches an ontology HEAD; the structural-fact route stays demonstrated.

D5 -- there was NO exclusion mechanism. Traced it: corpus membership is
recomputed on every read by link_nodes.gather() from what is physically under
runs/, filtered only by "is a module", `translated`, and "the .lp exists".
Waivers keep a module rather than drop one and act on the re-translation work
list; the graveyard is persistence only (graveyard.py:8); graph_corrections.py
never filters g["nodes"]. So a known-bad module could only be removed by
deleting evidence. corpus_exclusions.py closes that: a table of adjudicated
verdicts, each keyed to ONE artifact by sha256 and by the assert the verdict
was about, refusing (SystemExit) on any unmet precondition, and never an
id-level ban -- a later run's module for the same node enters normally and
needs its own adjudication. Nothing under runs/ is written, moved or deleted.

  l1108_1367_n014 -- the erotica/gore `permit` sourced from a `!!! meta
  "Commentary"` block three lines under an authority=system prohibition
  (EXPERIMENTS.md D1) -- is out. gather(): 207 -> 206. Its evidence is intact
  and pinned intact by test.

  ⚠️ NOT COVERED: _debug_gen11/stage4_baseline/stage4_driver.py carries a
  fenced COPY of the gather logic that does not consult the table. Left alone
  on purpose -- that file is another agent's live work.

   name goes in `requires`; the third exemplar shipped `"requires": []` while

   TOWARD ROUTING, not toward abstention: "There are three routes: a node
   stating a norm goes to `asserts`; a node stating a structural fact about the
   document goes to `ontology` with `asserts` empty; only a node that
   establishes neither is an abstention. An empty `asserts` list is not an empty
   module."
   ⛔ REJECTED BY NAME: making the example abstain on headings or on document

   `rule_under_heading/2`, which ZERO of 773 nodes provide, so it derived
   nothing while 126 nodes needed the `guideline_authority` it was meant to
   supply. Moved to `inputs` on the grounds of the node's OWN contract
   (`NEEDS: (none)`), never on "no provider turned up" -- which would be the
   convergent-repair attack `checks.py` warns about -- and carries a ⚠️ against
   generalising it into "move borrowed names to `inputs` to look clean".
   The second, independent reason not to abstain is now stated: `PROVIDES`
